#385432 14 2007-12-02T19:28:06Z Cossack
Quote: Atilla, November 29, 2007 02:00 am
This has been suggested several times before, and it's not going to happen.

Reasons:
- Anything other than MIDI would drastically increase the download size.
- N works fine with a media player in the background.
- Most people would prefer their own music to anything M&R would compose anyway. Especially if it's MIDI.
- The library of music would be very small. Consider the number of hours you've played N. Imagine a two-minute track repeating. Over. And over. And over.
- M&R's time is better spent on other things.

Glad thats sorted. Soundtrack would be pretty pointless, especially coz people listen to their own music in the mean time. altho there should be an animation of N doing somehting like dancing if he stands still for too long ...

Wow broad topic. I am Catholic (Yeah I said it) and this is what I believe.

I believe that Jesus is god's son and he is our savior. He was born by Mary and he came to earth by birth to show us that he could appear like everyone else. If he wanted he could have just appeared in the sky and done something big and flashy, but he choose not to. While on earth he gathered disciples and told them to gather followers of him. He then died on the cross to save people from all the sins that they committed and still are commiting today. Then he was buried descended to hell and then rose to heaven. He appeared before his disciples a couple of other times after his death on earth to lead them and tell them how they should go out and gather followers (Why does that sound so odd?). Quote: southpaw, 2007-12-04T23:09:39Z
uhhh.... no green isn't primary. Where did you learn that?

Green is taught as a primary colour in many science classes. This is because red and green light, when mixed, appear yellow. Also, most humans have three different types of colour receptor in their eye, which are respond most strongly to red, green, and blue light, respectively.

Yellow is taught as primary in art classes because red and green pigment generally don't mix well, whereas red and yellow and yellow and blue do.

Quote: Atilla, December 12, 2007 11:38 pm
I really can't stand the "against nature" argument.

Firstly, rape is the result of base instinct, and leads to normal reproduction. Totally natural. Now, I'm fairly sure most of the people who claim homosexuality is immoral because it's "against nature" would also consider rape to be immoral. We may also point to historical examples - interracial marriage and women with careers were also considered to be "against nature" not so long ago, and I think the vast majority of the population - at least in Western countries - now accepts both of these things. Thus, I believe it is fairly clear that what is "natural" doesn't correlate well with what is moral. In other words, there are plenty of "natural" things which are considered immoral and plenty of "unnatural" ones considered moral.

Secondly, I debate that homosexuality is against nature. You say that heterosexual reproduction is natural because it's a base instinct, and that's true - if you're heterosexual. The base instinct of a homosexual may well cause attraction to their own gender instead. Furthermore, contrary to what you have stated, homosexual behaviour is frequently observed in apes. I suggest you google it. Or look at Wikipedia.

interracial marridges and women with careers were accepted to be fine as if became beneficial to the wider population. less unemployed, more jobs taken by more suitable individuals. Homosexualtiy is not beneficial in any way. When you say rape is a result of base instinct - yes it is. but it dosnt automatically mean its right - and i never implied so. i did not mean there is just black and white. i never said that if you go by base instincts it automatically is right. i said that if you go against them then it isnt right either (for said topic).

many occasions of homosexuality are caused by a defficiency in a human/animal, be it mental or physical (eg - hormonal where too much of a certain hormone is produced). going by this - it is a break from the norm which is obviously superior. (purely because if homosexuality was norm - then there wouldnt be a great lot of creatures left alive after the 1st generation dies out)

Quote: blue_tetris, 2007-12-16T16:34:09Z
Lastly, to capt_weasle: An atheistic determinist sees the world as entirely governed by the interactions of atoms, forces, and natural laws. The mind isn't anything special; it's just a funny word for "brain". It's a lump of meat. It is full of axioms and dendrites, which fire synapses and respond to neurotransmitters. There are similar systems at work in the water cycle and in computers. Do these things have free will?

If a hammer is dropped in the air, there's nothing random to its motion. A physicist could calculate its path for you. A single cause ends with a single effect. We see this kind of predictability in our world every day, and it allows us to do engineering and make technological advances. If a single cause results in a single event, why should a single jolt of electricity across the surface of a human organ result in two possible outcomes? What makes the atoms in the brain different than the atoms in an hammer? Why does the electricity in one lump of matter decide where to go, while the electricity traveling through another would be utterly derpived of choice?

Why should an atheist view his or her organs as somehow exempt from the perfectly mathematical laws of nature? The earth doesn't decide to rotate, my refrigerator doesn't decide to cool my foods, my spleen doesn't decide to filter waste from my bloodstream, my brain doesn't decide to keep me home from work some Thursdays. These things happen because external and internal properties have resulted in specific outcomes. If I believe in a world where my brainmeat's atoms get freewill, it's perfectly logical for me to believe in a world where sofa atoms and umbrella atoms get freewill also.

#393399 20 2007-12-26T01:36:32Z Cossack
Quote: Goatman, December 25, 2007 02:18 am
Quote: Cossack, December 25, 2007 01:38 am
. a bunch of manakins lying on the floor with red pain over them. there were a tonn of other crap like it. its just not art. did the person try hard, learn for years and produce that with effort. no, he got a bunch of manakins, dropped them to the floor and then went and threw red paint over it. i could do it too, but instead when i was into art properly i went for the more conventional style of actual art as opposed to gimmics.
Yes, but you are forgetting two things.

Firstly, the piece you were looking at was most likely conceptual art. And most conceptual artists would say that anyone can learn the skills of painting styles ,ie. the impressionists or Van Gough, but not everyone can work out how to put a statement, message, or feeling into their art, and hence conceptual is made to give the viewer an insight in to the artists opinions, not just skills.


Quote: Cossack, 2007-12-25T01:38:06Z
thats an empty room with lights in it. its a waste of space. it inspires no feeling other than surprise to why someone actually let him use that space. its not art - i say again - its degeneration.

Secondly, about the above. Chances are Martin Creed would be rather happy that you are challenging his piece. As I said above, conceptual I believe is made to express an artists opinion. And so Martin Creed is trying to inspire a feeling of being used (paying to get into a gallery to see just bare walls), outrage (as you are obviously showing now), and inspiring critique. Also, to me this work is challenging the thought of where the artwork ends and the gallery begins; if something's in a gallery, does that make it art? And so if a gallery is within a gallery, is that gallery art?

And there is almost always a meaning behind an artwork. In fact, I have a challenge for you Cossack. Find a piece of art with absolutely no meaning. If you can, my argument will be smashed into a million pieces. But if you can't, yours will.

what you set me is an impossible task. you can stick a meaning to everything. i could piss in a bucket and display it in the tate modern itll pass off as my contempt for modern art. people would say that it shows powerful emotion of my hatred towars the art and maybe a concession that i cant get rid of it because i ended up putting it there, but my ultimate reason for it beign there may have been coz i wanted to piss a lot and the bucket was the closest thing to hand. i just left it in a room in the gallery, maybe left a sign to have a laugh.

what i say is. art is when you show feeling through skill. if the only thing you can bring across is hatred and annoyance from your art, it obviously has some problems with it. when a person shows that emotion through what he has worked on and tried ot achieve his whole life, a positive feeling will be induced.


also - to coceptual artists saying that anyone can learn the skill of painting styles - yes they can, but i never specified that i define art as any old picute. if i draw something - and im not too bad at it - i will probably not concider it art, but others might. feeling will always be brought across something that has been worked for. as opposed to my mentioned example of the red manakins. they inspire the wrong emotions. if that man however carved each of those manakins from stone and showd that there was effort and good result it would be better. maybe i wouldnt like it but i would respect that he has skill and could do something else if need be. what most of the modern art shows me is that a lot of people who dont have skill are exploiting it.

Quote: Ngaged, December 31, 2007 12:18 am
That's an interesting observation. I've kinda noticed that as well.

I have an idea as to how this has happened. Not sure if it's that accurate, but I'm just thinking. You know how we have all these levels or level packs created by some of the more well-known members of the community? A vast majority of those maps are more Action. These Action maps are then shown to potential beginners to the game who then start making millions of action maps. Thus, the numbers of Race maps decrease, because they are not aware of what a Race map consists of. This kind of effects the other playable categories of maps, like Puzzle and Survival. But going back to what was noted earlier, we've lost some of the mapmakers who specialized in Race maps, but we still have enough good Survival and Puzzle makers to keep those categories going. You can clearly see how Action is the more "dominant" category.

No idea if that made any sense to anyone, but it was worth a shot.

I'm not a good Race maker myself, so I can't help you guys make a reasonable Race map.

Justice is subjective and is what we make it to be. laws change to accomodate different situations and justice is therefore changed. some might feel that its wrong - but that justice has been decided by a majority if you will. when people resent this justice then things change again (my example of revolution). What i was trying to say is that there is not defined *justice*. you cant go to some place and look at a defenition of it. everything we have of justice is guidelines and worked from out moral compasses. Im not resenting the fact that the bible has a lot to do with the way morals are right now, but i am saying that its not the defenition of morals. other people can come to the same conclusions of killing being wrong and such without having to read the bible or even know of it.

as for grounds to contest the government - on the grounds of if you yourself feel hard done. if more people agree - it becomes more of a possiblity. if even more agree then you have the option to do something about it. thats why usually gvnt's try to accomodate for all people. there will always be some who dont like somehting and they think there is no justice but seeing as everyone does have a different take on justice its physically impossible to cater for all. The bible just put what people had reached themselves to paper. before the bible - societies had some morals too. they didnt walk onto the street and murder someone on a whim. ancient civilisations had their forms of justice without having the bible.

PS: dont feel isolated. there are other people who feel this way as i have no doubt, they just arnt arguing the point. only 2 or 3 are really arguing here (you, iangb, zz9 and myself - with some comments from others)

I will agree with you that we need an "absolute framework" for morality, but i believe that the discussion is where it comes from and how absolute. its still a framework that is there but it can change and does change. my argument is that originally - there is no moral guideline set in stone anywhere - we have made our own and have made things to enforce it. it is still liable to change depending on circumstance.
for example using the infant and pig - the morality of having humans above animals - where does it come from? the bible or from the nature and evolution of humans? i dont think that i would have needed a book/deity to tell me im above a beast for me to think so. But if suddenly i were to see that some animal made a house and used tools and communicated with loved ones etc... i would change my moral view of how much (if at all) better i am than them. the moral base is subject to change but mostly morality is universally accepted by people. it dosnt mean that the framework dosnt exist (i think my argument just seemed to imply i say so) its just that i believe morality to come from what humans have decided is the best for as many as possible.

as for justice - i implied for the majority, but that dosnt suddenly mean that its against the minority.

The civilisations that took on christianity only dropped those morals so they could intigrate. that does not mean that it was the right thing to do or that they liked it. i say this because there is nothing that would deem christianity better than another religion like islam. many would contest this but apart from trivial biased views - as someone with absolutely no religious ties whatsoever - i cannot see how one is better that the other.

so the excessive hype about half life 2 didnt bother you while halos did? i kinda see where youre coming from (its sort of more commercial and casual people as opposed to just gamers love it but still...) besides - halos story was a lot better than half lifes (altho thats my oppinion and yours is yours)
I got HL2 in 06/07 so i was never really affected by the hype. And it's not the hype about halo that bothers me it's the fact that people declare it gods gift to gamers, best fps ever etc. I mean Hl2 is definetly not the best ever but it is good. As is Halo.

As an example there is a Halo fanboy in my tutor group at college, i asked him what makes halo better than any other FPS and he couldn't come up with anything i couldn't counter. Although that may be because he is an idiot but... meh.

Halo was groundbreaking because it was the first FPS on XBOX to have one analog stick for aiming, and one for shooting. The story was great, the vehicles felt like they were naturally part of the environment, it had innovative new weapons and a great single player/co-op storyline. The shield system greatly enhanced gameplay, and was the inspiration for almost every modern FPS (though the first halo did have health). Most good shooters now don't have a health bar, but if you take too many hits in a row you'll drop. True, it isn't the best shooter ever made (Perfect Dark is.), but it's pretty damn far up there. As far as I'm concerned, it was the best thing ever to hit the XBOX.
FPS games on the PS1 did that years before so it's not exactly groundbreaking or inovating. Also i'd like to point out that halo is the first FPS released for the xbox. And yeah there's ntohing wrong with the haloverse i just think the games are mediocre.

@ lumiflare - well - I saw the hype since its release and after playing it promptly realised that it was nothing special. A LOT of people declare that half life 2 is the best shooter ever. and it just isnt. But over all i see your point and accept.

id like to point out that most good shooters do have a health bar (crysis, bioshock, stalker, fear, etc) with the possible exception of the Call of Duty games which are very good. Would also like to say that the story of halo is one of the better in an FPS game ive seen, but overall (despite loving the game) the gameplay wasnt the best. guns were fun but relied on clicking and hoping for the best as there was no aiming to speak of (i say clicking coz i playedthe pc version). halo was an early console shooter and what that means is that for a pc player the combat was a little dulled. It was "ground breaking" for the consoles, which again as a pc player i dont concider that special. The second 2 games made it worse imo coz they just copied the 1st in poorer ways. halo 1 got away with dreadful grafix as it had great story and fun weapons to push. Halo 2 & 3 had poor grafix for their times and are just copies of the 1st is somewhat different settings.
To generalise that point - halo - awsome game, but not best. halo sequels - average.

#410940 3 2008-02-20T18:56:09Z Cossack
Quote: wiki
Consequentialism refers to those moral theories which hold that the consequences of a particular action form the basis for any valid moral judgment about that action. Thus, from a consequentialist standpoint, a morally right action is one that produces a good outcome, or consequence.

For consequentialism then if the slaughter of 10 million people benifited the world - then it would be a morally right choice. Obviously there could be degrees to this (never just black and white) like if this slaughter would mean that a village of 50 would survive or that it would put an end to world hunger. if its the latter then it would still not be moral - if the former then it will be.

Quote: Infernus, February 22, 2008 10:30 am
Alcohol already negatively impacts on many facets of society. 21 is a definite minimum.

The human body only starts to decline in important physiological development in the 20's (in most situations) and alcohol consumed before then harms the body more dramatically. Alcohol should only be available after this development is complete.

Some may argue, however, that by these ages the decision to drink should be a personal one. Why then can't you buy heroin at the expense of your health? Yes, there is a difference in severity, but the concept is the same.

The social impacts alcohol have on youth are often hidden, as they may not surface physically. I have a friend who has not completed his schooling and will never meaningfully contribute to the community, solely due to alcohol. I have at least three other friends who have had their grades significantly affected by alcohol. Why make this problem worse?

the problem there is not with the age limit of the kids but with the culture and the idea they have of it. i grew up in a location and culture where people drink a lot, but in social situations and in ways that reduce the damage (people get together and drink, but have food, with it and drink in moderation and dont binge on it). naturally there are always people who get blootered, but mostly it was reasonable. so for these kids the idea of getting totally trashed should be removed. if the drink is given at an earlier age in small quantities by parents and generally they dont make it something of a taboo for the kids then alcohol wont be as desirable and kids wont try and show how grown up they can be by destroying themselves. its all about the upbringing, not the actual age.
